# Test-Coverage Manifest — Part 1 of 3

> ## Status: corrected 2026-08-11 — reviewable, not yet decided
>
> 57/57 files, 695 behaviour rows. `npm run test:audit-columns` passes on this
> file (0 failures, 0 warnings).
>
> **Resolved.** The first draft carried 417 cells reading "unknown — not yet
> searched" while 500 rows proposed a target presuming that search came back
> empty, plus 280 unqualified `none found` assertions. The correction pass
> supplied the full 44-file Worker test inventory and re-derived both columns.
> Result: **81 rows now cite a real Worker equivalent by path and line** — those
> behaviours already have coverage and would otherwise have been rewritten in
> Phase 3.2. Every remaining negative names the files that were searched.
>
> **Resolved 2026-08-12 — the ten bad citations.** Ten `searched:` entries named
> files that do not exist, and were left annotated rather than silently corrected,
> because substituting a real filename would assert a search that was never run.
> The searches have since actually been run and the cells now record what was
> found: `subscription-validation.test.ts` and `utils/auth.test.ts` cover
> subscription access-gating by status (`auth.test.ts:235` *allows* `past_due`
> rather than downgrading it), and no dunning or email-dispatch code exists
> anywhere in `workers/src` — so the seven dunning/email negatives hold on real
> evidence. The two mistyped dashboard references now point at
> `database.dashboard.pglite.node.test.ts`, and the expired-items reference now
> says plainly that no such Worker suite exists.
>
> **Also resolved: two sections under-covered their files.** `error-handler`
> (11 rows for 12 tests) and `expired-item.service` (13 for 14) each silently
> dropped a behaviour — `headersSent`/`next(err)` middleware semantics and
> SQLite connection release respectively, both now recorded as `retire`. Neither
> was visible to any check that existed when this file was first committed;
> `npm run test:audit-manifest` now catches this class.
>
> **One caveat that survives.**
>
> 1. **The eleven `database.backup.*` rows propose `retire`.** They describe
>    SQLite file-copy mechanics (backup directory creation, `DATABASE_PATH` copy)
>    which retire with the SQLite backend under any outcome. **This does not
>    settle where the backup capability lives** — that remains an open task 2.4
>    decision covering the route and the Neon-native / scheduled-R2 alternatives.
>
> **What the gate does and does not prove.**
> `scripts/check-audit-columns.js` proves no column is unfilled and no decision
> outruns its own evidence. It cannot prove a cited file supports the claim
> attached to it. That took reading the sources — which is how both the
> `expiry-date-parser` miss and the ten bad paths were found.
>
> Every row is `PROPOSED`. Deletion authority is human; no keep/retire call in
> this file is settled until reviewed.
>
> **Re-pointed 2026-08-28 by task 3.1.0 — four cited Worker test files were deleted.**
> `middleware.test.ts`, `middleware/auth.test.ts`, `middleware/require-role.test.ts` and
> `utils/auth.test.ts` were removed with the undeployed `index.ts` entry point (2.5 Finding 22).
> 246 rows across the four parts cited at least one of them. Two rules were applied.
> **215 rows** named a deleted file only inside a `none found (searched: ...)` list; those rows
> already recorded no equivalent, so only the search target was restated against live code
> (auth → `workers/src/index-minimal.ts` `authenticateApiRequest`:830; roles →
> `workers/src/constants/roles.test.ts`; rate limiting →
> `workers/src/utils/minimal-rate-limit.test.ts`) and **no decision changed**.
> **47 rows** cited a deleted file as a positive equivalent. Of those, 16 were repointed to live
> coverage that genuinely holds — role normalization to `constants/roles.test.ts`, and the
> admin-only gates to `minimal-api-routes.test.ts`, which task 3.1.0 extended to cover
> `handleClearSupplierPolicy` and `handleBulkAttachPolicy`. **31 were REOPENED**: their
> equivalence rested on tests for modules with no production importer, so the claim did not
> survive the deletion. They are marked `REOPENED by task 3.1.0` and carry their superseded
> decision inline. Three groups, none of them a re-pointing exercise:
> **22 auth/JWT rows** (the live Clerk path has no unit coverage for signature, expiry, tenant
> claim or subscription status, and `__tests__/auth-integration.test.ts` asserts
> `const expected = true` in 14 of its 17 cases);
> **2 role rows** for `requireOrgRole(admin, manager)`, which is live Express middleware on the
> user, store-area and organization-invite routes with no admin+manager equivalent anywhere in
> the Worker; and **3 rate-limit rows**, the limiter's reject path being untested in live code.
> These 25 need a decision from the owner; they are not settled.
>
> **Corrected during the same task.** A first pass recorded four further role rows
> (`requirePermission('upload_files')` ×3, `requireMinRole(manager)` ×1) as a live
> authorization gap, reading the Worker's absent upload role check as a regression against
> Express. That was half-verified and wrong: `backend/src/routes/upload.routes.ts` gates uploads
> with `authenticateToken` + `checkUsageLimit` + rate limiters and no role middleware, and
> `requirePermission` (`backend/src/middleware/requireOrgRole.ts:75`) and `requireMinRole`
> (`:110`) occur nowhere outside their own JSDoc examples at `:73` and `:107`. The permission
> model was built and tested on both sides and wired to nothing, so those four rows are
> `retire` — there is no behaviour to port. Upload authorization, if wanted, is net-new product
> work on a fresh decision, not a migration row.

**Task 2.2** — `retire-express-unify-on-postgres`

**Scope:** `backend/src/tests/unit/[a-i]*.test.ts` — 57 files (alphabetical a–i).
Parts 2–3 cover the remaining files; this manifest does not touch them.

**Status:** Every row carries a `PROPOSED:` prefix. This is an inventory, not a decision
record. The Express suite keeps running on SQLite until deletion; this manifest records what
its coverage maps onto in the Worker suite.

**Columns:** `Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision`

**Named gates:** `tenant-isolation`, `penetration`, `concurrency`, `feature-limits`,
`webhook-security`, `scheduled-job-idempotency`, `authorization-precedence`, `negative/error`,
or `—`.

**Targets:** `worker-equivalent-exists` | `worker-shaped-rewrite` | `retire`.

**Worker test inventory consulted:** `workers/src/**/*.test.ts` (43 files, enumerated in
full before writing rows). "none found" means no Worker test file name matches the behaviour
domain; it is based on the complete file list, not a guess.

### atomicity.test.ts

Tests atomic SKU/inventory counter updates via Prisma `$transaction`. These are
business-critical behaviours: atomic increment/decrement, rollback on failure, concurrent
counter safety, race-condition prevention, and plan-limit enforcement. Named gates
**concurrency** and **feature-limits** are directly exercised. The Worker uses Postgres
transactions (not Prisma `$transaction`), so the behaviour must be re-tested against the
Worker's transaction model — worker-shaped-rewrite. Worker equivalent not yet searched.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Atomically increments total_skus on product create | atomicity.test.ts:59 | concurrency | workers/src/database.usage-limits.pglite.node.test.ts:101 - "creates the product that sits exactly at the last free slot" asserts the row count reaches the cap | worker-equivalent-exists | The Worker keeps no usage counter. The tier cap is a `COUNT(*)` inside the INSERT (`workers/src/utils/usage-limits.ts` header), so there is no `total_skus` or `total_inventory_items` to corrupt, increment or decrement; the property that survives is that a create is counted, asserted here against real SQL | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists |
| Rolls back SKU counter if product creation fails | atomicity.test.ts:105 | negative/error | none - the Worker keeps no SKU counter; the cap is checked inside the INSERT, so a failed insert changes nothing | retire | Task 3.2 batch 5b. Express incremented a counter in a transaction with the insert and rolled it back on failure. The Worker has no counter: workers/src/database.usage-limits.pglite.node.test.ts:115 - "refuses the product that would exceed the cap, and writes nothing" shows a refused create writes no row | PROPOSED: retire - counter design removed |
| Handles concurrent SKU counter updates correctly | atomicity.test.ts:127 | concurrency | none found (searched: workers/src/utils/usage-limits.test.ts, workers/src/database.usage-limits.pglite.node.test.ts, workers/src/__tests__/usage-limits-integration.test.ts, workers/src/__tests__/catalogue-import-upsert.node.test.ts, workers/src/database.worklist.pglite.node.test.ts) | retire | The Worker keeps no usage counter. The tier cap is a `COUNT(*)` inside the INSERT (`workers/src/utils/usage-limits.ts` header), so there is no `total_skus` or `total_inventory_items` to corrupt, increment or decrement. ### auth.middleware.di-boundary.test.ts

Tests that Express `authenticateToken` middleware resolves Clerk-token users and
subscriptions through DI repositories (`tsyringe`) without calling the database factory
directly. This is an Express/tsyringe DI-boundary architectural test. The Worker has its own
auth middleware (the deleted `middleware/auth.test.ts`, removed with the dead index.ts layer in task 3.1.0) but does not use tsyringe DI. The
DI-boundary assertion is Express-specific; the core Clerk-token resolution behaviour may be
covered by Worker auth tests (not yet verified).

### clerk-webhook-soft-delete.test.ts

Tests `ClerkWebhookService` soft-delete behavior using a real Prisma client against SQLite.
The `organizationMembership.deleted` webhook event soft-deletes the user (sets `deletedAt`)
rather than hard-deleting, and audit logs are preserved with `SET NULL` on the user FK.
These are **webhook-security** behaviours. The Worker has
`workers/src/clerk/bootstrap-handler.node.test.ts` which may cover webhook handling, but it
has not been verified.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |

Working it also found that a membership-deleted event naming an organization this database has never seen fell through to an unscoped UPDATE and soft-deleted the user in whatever organization they belong to. Fixed in `workers/src/clerk/clerk-persistence.ts` and pinned by "does not retire a member because the event names an organization this database has never seen"; scoping to the named organization is mutation-verified by "ignores a departure from an organization the member has since left for another" | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists - defect found and fixed while working this row |

### credit-claim.job.test.ts

Tests scheduled jobs (`runCreditClaimReminderJob`, `runCreditClaimPhotoPurgeJob`) that iterate
over organizations and process credit-claim reminders and photo purges. These are
**scheduled-job-idempotency** and **tenant-isolation** named gates. The Worker will rehome
these to Cron Triggers / Queues per Phase 3.3.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sends follow-up for each due claim, counts failures without stopping | credit-claim.job.test.ts:25 | scheduled-job-idempotency | workers/src/scheduled/jobs/credit-claim-follow-ups.pglite.node.test.ts:75 - "enqueues one message per due claim across organizations" | worker-equivalent-exists | The Worker job enqueues one queue message per due claim and the consumer sends. A failing send is retried by the queue rather than counted and skipped in-process, so "counts failures without stopping" has no direct analogue | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists |
| Processes every organization | credit-claim.job.test.ts:41 | scheduled-job-idempotency | workers/src/scheduled/jobs/credit-claim-follow-ups.pglite.node.test.ts:75 - "enqueues one message per due claim across organizations" seeds claims in two organizations | worker-equivalent-exists | The job lists due claims across all organizations in one query | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists |
| Sums purged photos across organizations | credit-claim.job.test.ts:56 | scheduled-job-idempotency | workers/src/scheduled/scheduled.pglite.node.test.ts:322 - "lists due photos across organizations, skipping not-due and NULL rows"; workers/src/scheduled/jobs/credit-claim-photo-purge.test.ts:70 - "deletes the R2 object then the row for each due photo" | worker-equivalent-exists | There is no per-organization loop to sum over: one query lists due photos and the job deletes each | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists |
| Keeps purging later orgs when one org throws (isolation) | credit-claim.job.test.ts:67 | scheduled-job-idempotency | workers/src/scheduled/jobs/credit-claim-photo-purge.test.ts:120 - "captures a row-delete failure per photo, continues, and marks the run failed" | worker-equivalent-exists | Isolation is per photo, which is finer than Express's per organization; a failing R2 delete is covered by the sibling test | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists |

### credit-claim.service.test.ts

Tests `CreditClaimService` with mocked dependencies (repos, email sender, storage, Prisma
`$transaction`). Exercises the full claim lifecycle: build, send, follow-up, record outcome,
photo upload, and photo purge. Named gates include **penetration** (filename sanitization),
**concurrency** (race condition prevention), and many **negative/error** cases. The Worker
has `workers/src/database.credit-claim.conformance.node.test.ts` which may cover some
behaviours, but not verified.

apply with data preservation, uniqueness enforcement, and enum CHECK constraints. Prisma
schema checks and backend-relative migration file checks retire with the backend. The SQLite
migration apply test verifies constraints (uniqueness, enums) that the Postgres migration
fingerprint tests (Phase 1.3) cover at the catalog level.

(duplicate detection), and business rules (expiry date limits, cost price validation). Uses
mocked SQLite `getDb`/`releaseDb`. The middleware is Express-specific, but the underlying
validation behaviours (referential integrity, duplicate prevention, business rule enforcement)

with mocked Prisma and DI container. Named gates include **feature-limits** (tier-based
feature access, usage limits, 80% warning), **authorization-precedence** (missing
organizationId rejection), and **negative/error** (database errors, missing records). The
Worker had `middleware/require-role.test.ts`, deleted with the dead index.ts layer in task 3.1.0; live role coverage is now `workers/src/constants/roles.test.ts`,
but feature-gating logic must be re-tested on the Worker.

Tests `checkUsageLimit` creation-lock behaviour — returns 403 with `locked: true` for POST
when org is creation-locked, checks usage through repositories instead of default DB client,
and allows PUT when creation-locked so customers can reduce usage. Named gates include
**feature-limits** (creation lock) and **authorization-precedence** (repository-based access).

### inventory-tenant-filtering.test.ts

Tests `InventoryService` tenant filtering with a real Prisma client and SQLite test database.
Creates two organizations (org1, org2) with their own products, locations, and inventory
items, then verifies that each org's `InventoryService` instance only sees its own items.
This is a critical **tenant-isolation** named gate — cross-tenant access must be prevented
on the Worker. Uses Prisma+SQLite directly; the tenant isolation behaviour must be re-tested

---

### inventory.service.test.ts

Tests `InventoryService` with mocked Prisma and injected repositories. Covers markdown price
calculation with credit-scope matrices, create/update/delete with organization filtering,
repository delegation, auto-calculate markdown status, transaction logging, and sync markdown
status calculation. All Prisma-based mechanics retire with the backend, but the business
behaviour — tenant-scoped CRUD, credit-scope markdown pricing, audit logging, usage counter
updates, and markdown status calculation — must be re-tested on the Worker.

### item-transaction.model.test.ts

Tests `item-transaction.model` SQLite helpers — `itemTransactionsTable` metadata and
`createItemTransaction` — with mocked `getDb`/`prepare`/`run`. Uses SQLite-specific SQL,
`lastInsertRowid` BigInt-to-number conversion, and snake_case column naming. The Worker uses
PGlite/Postgres with different SQL and column naming. The SQLite model mechanics retire with
the backend; the transaction-creation contract must be re-tested on the Worker.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Exposes table metadata used by migration helpers (name, columns, creationSchema) | item-transaction.model.test.ts:22 | — | none found (searched: workers/src/database.conformance.node.test.ts, workers/src/database.disposition.pglite.node.test.ts) — Worker uses Postgres schema managed by migrations, no SQLite table metadata export | retire | SQLite table metadata for migrations; Worker uses Postgres schema | PROPOSED: retire — SQLite migration metadata with no Worker analogue |
| Inserts a transaction row and returns numeric last insert id (BigInt conversion) | item-transaction.model.test.ts:38 | — | none found (searched: workers/src/database.conformance.node.test.ts, workers/src/database.disposition.pglite.node.test.ts) — no Worker test covers item-transaction model creation with SQLite lastInsertRowid | worker-shaped-rewrite | createItemTransaction with SQLite lastInsertRowid | PROPOSED: worker-shaped-rewrite — transaction creation must be re-tested on Worker |
| Handles numeric sqlite row ids without conversion issues | item-transaction.model.test.ts:60 | — | none found (searched: workers/src/database.conformance.node.test.ts, workers/src/database.disposition.pglite.node.test.ts) — no Worker test covers item-transaction model creation with SQLite lastInsertRowid | worker-shaped-rewrite | numeric row id handling | PROPOSED: worker-shaped-rewrite — transaction creation must be re-tested on Worker |
